\mathcal{R}_{n}\geq\frac{1}{2N}\inf_{\hat{b}}\sup_{b\in B}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[d_{H}\left(\hat{b},b\right)\right]\geq\frac{1}{2N}\inf_{\hat{b}\in\mathcal{D}}\sup_{b\in\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[d_{H}\left(\hat{b},b\right)\right]

(A.35)

for any subset
\mathcal{D}\subseteq B
. We now choose
\mathcal{D}
carefully. Note that for any
N\geq 4V
(i.e.
N\geq 2p
), we can choose
\mathcal{D}
such that (Lemma 8 of
[
25
]
):

1.

d_{H}\left(b,b^{\prime}\right)>\frac{V}{2}
for all
b\neq b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}
.

2.

\log{|\mathcal{D}|}\geq\rho V\log{\left(\frac{N}{V}\right)}
with
\rho=0.233
.

Using this we modify equation (
A.35
) as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{n}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2N}\inf_{\hat{b}\in\mathcal{D}}\sup_{b\in\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[d_{H}\left(\hat{b},b\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2N}\inf_{\hat{b}\in\mathcal{D}}\sup_{b\in\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{E}_{b}\left[d_{H}\left(\hat{b},b\right)\mathds{1}_{\hat{b}\neq b}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{V}{4N}\inf_{\hat{b}\in\mathcal{D}}\sup_{b\in\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\hat{b}\neq b\right)\hskip 14.45377pt[\text{From point 1. above}]

\displaystyle=\frac{V}{4N}\inf_{\hat{b}\in\mathcal{D}}\sup_{b\in\mathcal{D}}\left(1-\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\hat{b}=b\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{V}{4N}\inf_{\hat{b}\in\mathcal{D}}\left(1-\min_{b\in\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\hat{b}=b\right)\right)

(A.36)

Now to further bound the above equation, we use the following lemma (see
[
8
]
):

Lemma A.9
.

Let
m\geq 1
,
(P_{i})_{0\leq i\leq m}
be a family of probability distributions and
(A_{i})_{0\leq i\leq m}
be a family of disjoint events. Let
a=\min_{0\leq i\leq m}P_{i}(A_{i})
. Then setting:

\bar{\mathscr{K}}=\frac{1}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\mathscr{K}(P_{i},P_{0})

where
\mathscr{K}
is the Kullback-Liebler divergence, we have:

a\leq\alpha\vee\left(\frac{\bar{\mathscr{K}}}{\log{(1+m)}}\right)\,.

where
\alpha=0.71
.

We now use this bound in equation (
A.36
). Fix any
b_{0}\in\mathcal{D}
. Define
A_{i}=\left\{\hat{b}=b_{i}\right\}
for all
b_{i}\in\mathcal{D}
which are disjoint events. Hence using Lemma
A.9
we obtain:

\min_{b\in\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\hat{b}=b\right)\leq\alpha\vee\left(\frac{\bar{\mathscr{K}}}{\log{|\mathcal{D}|}}\right)

(A.37)

where:

\bar{\mathscr{K}}=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}|-1}\sum_{b\in\mathcal{D},b\neq b_{0}}\mathscr{K}\left(P_{b}^{\otimes n},P_{b_{0}}^{\otimes n}\right)=\frac{n}{|\mathcal{D}|-1}\sum_{b\in\mathcal{D},b\neq b_{0}}\mathscr{K}\left(P_{b},P_{b_{0}}\right)\,.

Now note that for any
d_{1},d_{2}
we have:

\displaystyle\mathscr{K}\left(P_{d_{1}},P_{d_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X}\left[\mathscr{K}\left(P_{d_{1}}(Y\mid X),P_{d_{2}}(Y\mid X)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X}\left[\left(\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d_{1}>0}-\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d_{2}>0}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X}\left[\left|\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d_{1}>0}-\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d_{2}>0}\right|\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\left|\mathds{1}_{x_{i}^{\top}d_{1}>0}-\mathds{1}_{x_{i}^{\top}d_{2}>0}\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}d_{H}(b_{d_{1}},b_{d_{2}})\,.

Also by definition for any two
b,b^{\prime}\in\mathcal{D}
we have
d_{H}(b,b^{\prime})\leq 2V
. Hence we have:

\bar{\mathscr{K}}\leq\frac{2Vn}{N}\,.

This bound along with equation (
A.37
) modifies the bound of equation (
A.36
) as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{n}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{V}{4N}\inf_{\hat{b}\in\mathcal{D}}\left(1-\min_{b\in\mathcal{D}}\mathbb{P}_{b}\left(\hat{b}=b\right)\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{V}{4N}\left(1-\left(\alpha\vee\frac{2Vn}{N\log{|\mathcal{D}|}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{V(1-\alpha)}{4N}

(A.38)

when,

\displaystyle\alpha\geq\frac{2Vn}{N\log{|\mathcal{D}|}}\,.

which holds if:

\displaystyle\alpha>\frac{2Vn}{N\rho V\log{(N/V)}}

i.e. if:

N\log{\left(\frac{N}{V}\right)}\geq\frac{2n}{\alpha\rho}\,.

(A.39)

which is satisfied, if for example:

N=\left\lfloor\frac{4n}{\rho\alpha\left(1+\log{\left(\frac{n}{V}\right)}\right)}\right\rfloor.

Using this in equation (
A.38
) we conclude:

\mathcal{R}_{n}\gtrsim\frac{V}{n}\left(1+\log{\left(\frac{n}{V}\right)}\right)\asymp\frac{p}{n}\left(1+\log{\left(\frac{n}{p}\right)}\right)\,.

as
V=\lfloor p/2\rfloor
. We finally need to verify
N>4V
and that it satisfies equation (
A.39
). The first one is obviously true for all large
n
as
n/p\to\infty
. For the second one, lets forget the
\lfloor\cdot\rfloor
in the definition of
N
for the time being as it will not affect asymptotically. Then:

\displaystyle N\log{\left(\frac{N}{V}\right)}\geq\frac{2n}{\alpha\rho}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\frac{4n}{\rho\alpha\left(1+\log{\left(\frac{n}{V}\right)}\right)}\log{\left(\frac{4n}{V\rho\alpha\left(1+\log{\left(\frac{n}{V}\right)}\right)}\right)}\geq\frac{2n}{\alpha\rho}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left(1+\log{\left(\frac{n}{V}\right)}\right)}\log{\left(\frac{4n}{V\rho\alpha\left(1+\log{\left(\frac{n}{V}\right)}\right)}\right)}\geq\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\iff

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left(1+\log{\left(\frac{n}{V}\right)}\right)}\left[\log{\left(\frac{n}{V}\right)}+\log{\left(\frac{4}{\rho\alpha}\right)}-\log{\left(1+\log{\left(\frac{n}{V}\right)}\right)}\right]\geq\frac{1}{2}

As
n/V\to\infty
, LHS converges to 1 and eventually
>1/2
. Therefore the choice of
N
is valid for all large
n
.

A.8
Proof of Theorem
3.10

We first assume that Assumption
3.5
holds globally and our parameter space
\Omega=\Omega_{\alpha}\times\Omega_{\beta}
for
(\alpha_{0},\beta_{0})
is such that:

\min_{\alpha\in\Omega_{\alpha}}|\alpha-\beta_{0}|\wedge\min_{\beta\in\Omega_{\beta}}|\beta-\alpha_{0}|\geq\delta>0\,.

This is just to avoid the issue of consistency. One can relax this assumption with an additionally showing that the estimators are consistent. To prove Theorem
3.10
we use Theorem A.3 of
[
24
]
. To match our notation with that theorem, here our loss function
\gamma(\theta,\cdot)
is:

\displaystyle\gamma(\theta,(X,\xi))

\displaystyle=\tilde{H}_{k}\left(\xi+\alpha_{0}\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d_{0}\leq 0}+\beta_{0}\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d_{0}>0}-\alpha\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d\leq 0}-\beta\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d>0}\right)-\tilde{H}_{k}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\left(\tilde{H}_{k}\left(\xi+\alpha_{0}-\alpha\right)-\tilde{H}_{k}(\xi)\right)\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d\vee X^{\top}d_{0}\leq 0}

\displaystyle\qquad\left(\tilde{H}_{k}\left(\xi+\alpha_{0}-\beta\right)-\tilde{H}_{k}(\xi)\right)\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d_{0}\leq 0<X^{\top}d}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\left(\tilde{H}_{k}\left(\xi+\beta_{0}-\alpha\right)-\tilde{H}_{k}(\xi)\right)\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d\leq 0<X^{\top}d_{0}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\left(\tilde{H}_{k}\left(\xi+\beta_{0}-\beta\right)-\tilde{H}_{k}(\xi)\right)\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d\wedge X^{\top}d_{0}>0}

It is immediate from the definition that
\gamma(\theta_{0},(X,\xi))=0
. Also from equation (
A.15
) we know
\gamma(\theta,\cdot)
is uniform bounded by some constant only depending on
k
and the width of
\Omega
. Following similar arguments used in the proof of Theorem
3.6
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\ell(\theta,\theta_{0})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\gamma(\theta,(X,\xi))]\geq c_{k}\ {\sf dist}^{2}(\theta,\theta_{0})\,,

(A.40)

for some constant
c_{k}
(independent of
n
), where the
{\sf dist}
function is:

{\sf dist}(\theta,\theta_{0})=\sqrt{(\alpha-\alpha_{0})^{2}+(\beta-\beta_{0})^{2}+\mathbb{P}\left(\text{sign}(X^{\top}d)\neq\text{sign}(X^{\top}d_{0})\right)}\,.

Moreover, from equation (
A.15
) we have:

\displaystyle{\sf var}\left(\gamma(\theta,(X,\xi))\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[\gamma^{2}(\theta,(X,\xi))]\leq C_{k}\ {\sf dist}^{2}(\theta,\theta_{0})\,.

Hence this semi-metric
{\sf dist}
satisfies conditions of Theorem A.3 of
[
24
]
with
\omega(x)=x
. Next we need to bound the modulus of continuity:

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta:f_{\theta}\in\mathcal{F}_{m}\\
{\sf dist}(\theta,\theta_{m})\leq{\epsilon}\end{subarray}}\left|\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P\right)\left(\gamma(\theta,(X,\xi))-\gamma(\theta_{m},(X,\xi))\right)\right|\right]

Note that another application of equation (
A.15
) yields:

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta:f_{\theta}\in\mathcal{F}_{m}\\
{\sf dist}(\theta,\theta_{m})\leq{\epsilon}\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\gamma(\theta,(X,\xi))-\gamma(\theta_{m},(X,\xi))\right)^{2}\right]\lesssim{\epsilon}^{2}\,.

Hence applying Theorem 8.7 of
[
26
]
we have:

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta:f_{\theta}\in\mathcal{F}_{m}\\
{\sf dist}(\theta,\theta_{m})\leq{\epsilon}\end{subarray}}\left\|\left(\mathbb{P}_{n}-P\right)\left(\gamma(\theta,(X,\xi))-\gamma(\theta_{m},(X,\xi))\right)\right\|\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim{\epsilon}\sqrt{V_{m}\log{\frac{1}{{\epsilon}}}}\vee\frac{V_{m}}{\sqrt{n}}\log{\frac{1}{{\epsilon}}}

\displaystyle:=\psi_{m}({\epsilon})\,.

(A.41)

So a value of
{\epsilon}_{m}
that satisfies
\sqrt{n}{\epsilon}_{m}^{2}\geq\phi_{m}({\epsilon}_{m})
is:

{\epsilon}_{m}=\frac{V_{m}}{n}\log{\frac{n}{V_{m}}}\,.

Therefore we can take
x_{m}=V_{m}\log{(n/V_{m})}
and as
\omega(x)=x
we have
b(n)=1
(see Theorem A.3 of
[
24
]
for the exact expression of
\omega(x),b(n)
). Note that, as we are assuming
(s\log{p})/n\to 0
(Assumption
3.9
), it is sufficient to search among the models with
1\leq m\leq C\lfloor(n/\log{p})\rfloor
for any constant
C
. We take
C=1/4
here. With these choices, the value of
\Sigma
(as defined in Theorem A.3 of
[
24
]
) is:

\displaystyle\Sigma

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{1}{4}\lfloor\frac{n}{\log{p}}\rfloor}e^{-V_{i}\log{\frac{n}{V_{i}}}}

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{n\to\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{\frac{1}{4}\lfloor\frac{n}{\log{p}}\rfloor}e^{-V_{i}\log{\frac{n}{V_{i}}}}<\infty\,.

Hence, an application Theorem A.3 of
[
24
]
yields:

\mathbb{P}\left(\ell(\hat{\theta},\theta_{0})>C\text{pen}(s)+t\frac{C_{1}}{n}\right)\leq\Sigma e^{-t}\,.

This along with the value of
\text{pen}(s)
from equation (
3.4
) of the main paper and the lower bound equation (
A.40
) completes the first part of the proof, i.e.

{\sf dist}^{2}\left((\hat{\alpha}_{\text{init}},\hat{\beta}_{\text{init}},\hat{d}),(\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},d_{0})\right)=O_{p}\left(\frac{V_{s}}{n}\log{\frac{n}{V_{s}}}\right)\,.

The acceleration of the rate of
\hat{\alpha},\hat{\beta}
via replacing
d_{0}
by
\hat{d}
in the model equation is exactly same as that of Theorem
3.6
and hence skipped.

A.9
Proof of Theorem
3.14

Here again we assume for technical simplicity that the wedge assumption (Assumption
3.5
) is valid on entire
S^{p-1}
, although all our arguments can be extended to the case where the assumption is valid only locally along with a separate argument for the consistency of the estimator. We use the same notations as of Theorem
3.10
through out the proof. Define

y^{2}_{m}=2\kappa\frac{V_{m}\|\xi\|_{n,2}}{n}\log{\frac{n}{V_{m}}}

for all
m\in\mathcal{M}
, where
V_{m}
is the VC dimension of model
m
. For any such model
m
we obtain
\hat{\theta}_{m}
as:

\displaystyle\hat{\theta}_{m}

\displaystyle={\arg\min}_{\theta\in\Omega\times S^{p-1}_{m}}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left\{(Y-\alpha\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d\leq 0}-\beta\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d>0})^{2}-\xi^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle={\arg\min}_{\theta\in\Omega\times S^{p-1}_{m}}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left\{\xi\left(\alpha_{0}\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d_{0}\leq 0}+\beta_{0}\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d_{0}>0}-\alpha\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d\leq 0}-\beta\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d>0}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\frac{1}{2}\left(\alpha_{0}\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d_{0}\leq 0}+\beta_{0}\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d_{0}>0}-\alpha\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d\leq 0}-\beta\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d>0}\right)^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle:={\arg\min}_{\theta\in\Omega\times S^{p-1}_{m}}\mathbb{P}_{n}f_{\theta}

\displaystyle:={\arg\min}_{\theta\in\Omega\times S^{p-1}_{m}}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left(f_{\theta,1}+f_{\theta,2}\right)

where the functions
f_{d,1},f_{d,2}
are defined as:

\displaystyle f_{\theta,1}

\displaystyle=\xi\left(\alpha_{0}\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d_{0}\leq 0}+\beta_{0}\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d_{0}>0}-\alpha\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d\leq 0}-\beta\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d>0}\right)\,,

\displaystyle f_{\theta,2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\alpha_{0}\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d_{0}\leq 0}+\beta_{0}\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d_{0}>0}-\alpha\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d\leq 0}-\beta\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d>0}\right)^{2}\,.

The loss function used here is:

\ell(\theta,\theta_{0})=\mathbb{P}f_{\theta}\gtrsim{\sf dist}^{2}(\theta,\theta_{0})\,,

for all
\theta\in\Omega\in S^{p-1}
via the global version of Assumption
3.5
.
From the definition of
\hat{m}
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}f_{\hat{\theta}_{\hat{m}}}+\text{pen}(\hat{m})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{n}f_{\hat{\theta}_{s_{0}}}+\text{pen}(s_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}_{n}f_{\theta_{s_{0}}}+\text{pen}(s_{0}):=\text{pen}(s_{0})\,.

Using this we can bound the loss function:

\displaystyle\ell(\hat{\theta}_{\hat{m}},\theta_{0})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}f_{\hat{\theta}_{\hat{m}}}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\right)f_{\hat{\theta}_{\hat{m}}}+\mathbb{P}_{n}f_{\hat{\theta}_{\hat{m}}}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\right)f_{\hat{\theta}_{\hat{m}}}+\mathbb{P}_{n}f_{\hat{\theta}_{\hat{m}}}+\text{pen}(\hat{m})-\text{pen}(\hat{m})

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\right)f_{\hat{\theta}_{\hat{m}}}+\text{pen}(s_{0})-\text{pen}(\hat{m})

\displaystyle=\frac{\left(\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\right)f_{\hat{\theta}_{\hat{m}}}}{\ell(\hat{\theta}_{\hat{m}},\theta_{0})+y_{\hat{m}}^{2}}\left(\ell(\hat{\theta}_{\hat{m}},\theta_{0})+y_{\hat{m}}^{2}\right)+\text{pen}(s_{0})-\text{pen}(\hat{m})

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\theta\in\Omega\times S^{p-1}_{\hat{m}}}\frac{\left|\left(\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\right)f_{\theta}\right|}{\ell(\theta,\theta_{0})+y_{\hat{m}}^{2}}\left(\ell(\hat{\theta}_{\hat{m}},d_{0})+y_{\hat{m}}^{2}\right)+\text{pen}(s_{0})-\text{pen}(\hat{m})

For the rest of the calculation, define:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{m}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\theta\in\Omega\times S^{p-1}_{m}}\frac{\left|\left(\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\right)f_{\theta}\right|}{\ell(\theta,\theta_{0})+y_{m}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\theta\in\Omega\times S^{p-1}_{m}}\frac{\left|\left(\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\right)(f_{\theta,1}+f_{\theta,2})\right|}{\ell(\theta,\theta_{0})+y_{m}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\theta\in\Omega\times S^{p-1}_{m}}\frac{\left|\left(\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\right)f_{\theta,1}\right|}{\ell(\theta,\theta_{0})+y_{m}^{2}}+\sup_{\theta\in\Omega\times S^{p-1}_{m}}\frac{\left|\left(\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\right)f_{\theta,2}\right|}{\ell(\theta,\theta_{0})+y_{m}^{2}}

\displaystyle:=\Gamma_{m,1}+\Gamma_{m,2}\,.

Next we try to bound
\Gamma_{\hat{m}}
. More specifically, we bound
\Gamma_{m}
for all
m
and then use a union bound to bound
\Gamma_{\hat{m}}
. Note that, as the function class under the consideration of
\Gamma_{m,2}
is bounded we can use similar as of the proof of Theorem A.3 of
[
24
]
(i.e. applying Talagrand’s inequality and then bound the expectation and variance) to conclude:

\mathbb{P}\left(\Gamma_{\hat{m},2}\geq 1/4\right)=o(1)\,.

(A.42)

For
\Gamma_{m,1}
, we first decompose it as follows:

\displaystyle\Gamma_{m,1}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{d\in S^{p-1}_{m}}\frac{\left|\left(\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\right)\left(f_{\theta,1}-f_{\theta_{m},1}\right)\right|}{\ell(\theta,\theta_{0})+y_{m}^{2}}+\sup_{\theta\in\Omega\times S^{p-1}_{m}}\frac{\left|\left(\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\right)f_{\theta_{m},1}\right|}{\ell(\theta,\theta_{0})+y_{m}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{\theta\in\Omega\times S^{p-1}_{m}}\frac{\left|\left(\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\right)\left(f_{\theta,1}-f_{\theta_{m},1}\right)\right|}{\ell(\theta,\theta_{0})+y_{m}^{2}}+\frac{\left|\left(\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\right)f_{\theta_{m},1}\right|}{\ell(\theta_{m},\theta_{0})+y_{m}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{m,11}+\Gamma_{m,12}\,.

Bounding
\mathbb{E}[\Gamma_{m,12}]
is straight-forward:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\Gamma_{m,12}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{{\sf var}\left(f_{\theta_{m},1}\right)}}{\sqrt{n}\left(\ell(\theta_{m},\theta_{0})+y_{m}^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\xi\|_{2}{\sf dist}(\theta_{m},\theta_{0})}{\sqrt{n}\left({\sf dist}^{2}(\theta_{m},\theta_{0})+y_{m}^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\xi\|_{2}}{\sqrt{n}}\ \sup_{x\geq 0}\frac{x}{x^{2}+y_{m}^{2}}\leq\frac{\|\xi\|_{2}}{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}y_{m}}\,.

To bound
\mathbb{E}[\Gamma_{m,1}]
we use the maximal inequality of the weighted empirical process (see Lemma A.5 of
[
23
]
), which is a variant of peeling argument. First of all note that, by symmetrization and applying Theorem 8.7 of
[
26
]
, we have for any
1\leq k\leq n
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta\in\Omega\times S^{p-1}_{m}\\
{\sf dist}(\theta,\theta_{m})\leq{\epsilon}\end{subarray}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{k}\xi_{i}\left(\alpha_{m}\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d_{m}\leq 0}+\beta_{m}\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d_{m}>0}-\alpha\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d\leq 0}-\beta\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d>0}\right)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\left(\sigma_{{\epsilon}}\sqrt{kV_{m}\log{\frac{1}{\sigma_{{\epsilon}}}}}\vee V_{m}\log{\frac{1}{\sigma_{\epsilon}}}\right)\,.

where the
wimpy variance

\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}
is defined as:

\displaystyle\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta:f_{\theta}\in\mathcal{F}_{m}\\
{\sf dist}(\theta,\theta_{m})\leq{\epsilon}\end{subarray}}\sigma_{\xi}^{2}\ \mathbb{E}\left[\left(\alpha_{m}\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d_{m}\leq 0}+\beta_{m}\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d_{m}>0}-\alpha\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d\leq 0}-\beta\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d>0}\right)^{2}\right]\lesssim{\epsilon}^{2}\,.

This, along with Proposition
A.7
implies:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta:f_{\theta}\in\mathcal{F}_{m}\\
{\sf dist}(\theta,\theta_{m})\leq{\epsilon}\end{subarray}}\left|\left(\mathbb{P}-\mathbb{P}_{n}\right)\left(f_{\theta,1}-f_{\theta_{m},1}\right)\right|\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\theta:f_{\theta}\in\mathcal{F}_{m}\\
{\sf dist}(\theta,\theta_{m})\leq{\epsilon}\end{subarray}}\left|\sum_{i=1}^{k}\xi_{i}\left(\alpha_{m}\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d_{m}\leq 0}+\beta_{m}\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d_{m}>0}-\alpha\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d\leq 0}-\beta\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d>0}\right)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\left\|\xi\right\|_{2,1}{\epsilon}\sqrt{\frac{V_{m}}{n}\log{\left(\frac{1}{{\epsilon}}\right)}}+2\frac{V_{m}}{n}\log{\left(\frac{1}{{\epsilon}}\right)}\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\left|\xi_{i}\right|\right]:=\frac{\psi_{m}({\epsilon})}{\sqrt{n}}\,.

An application of Lemma A.5 of
[
23
]
yields:

\mathbb{E}[\Gamma_{m,1}]\lesssim\frac{\psi_{m}(2\sqrt{2}y_{m})}{\sqrt{n}y_{m}^{2}}

which, in turn, yields:

\displaystyle E(\Gamma_{m,1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\phi_{m}(2\sqrt{2}y_{m})}{\sqrt{n}y^{2}_{m}}+\frac{\|\xi\|_{2}}{2\sqrt{n}y_{m}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4\phi_{m}(2\sqrt{2}\epsilon_{m})}{\sqrt{n}y_{m}\epsilon_{m}}+\frac{\|\xi\|_{2}}{2\sqrt{n}y_{m}}\sqrt{\frac{y_{m}^{2}}{y^{2}_{m}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\sqrt{2}\phi_{m}(\epsilon_{m})}{\sqrt{n}y_{m}\epsilon_{m}}+\frac{\|\xi\|_{2}}{2\sqrt{n}y_{m}}\sqrt{\frac{\phi^{2}_{m}\left(y_{m}\right)}{y^{2}_{m}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\sqrt{2}\epsilon_{m}}{y_{m}}+\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}y_{m}}\sqrt{\frac{\phi^{2}_{m}\left(\epsilon_{m}\right)}{\epsilon^{2}_{m}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{\sqrt{\kappa}}+\frac{\|\xi\|_{2}}{2\sqrt{n}y_{m}}\frac{\phi_{m}\left(\epsilon_{m}\right)}{\epsilon_{m}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{\sqrt{\kappa}}+\frac{\|\xi\|_{2}\epsilon_{m}}{2y_{m}}\leq\frac{8+\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{\kappa}}

(A.43)

which can be made arbitrarily small by making
\kappa
arbitrarily large. Next, we bound the fluctuation of
V_{m,1}
around its mean using Chebychev inequality:

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\Gamma_{m,1}-\mathbb{E}[\Gamma_{m,1}]\right|\geq t\right)\leq\frac{{\sf var}(\Gamma_{m,1})}{t^{2}}\,.

To bound the variance we use Theorem 11.17 along with Theorem 11.1 of
[
9
]
. To match with their notation for the ease of the readers, we have:

\displaystyle X_{i,\theta}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left[\frac{\xi_{i}\left(\alpha_{m}\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d_{m}\leq 0}+\beta_{m}\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d_{m}>0}-\alpha\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d\leq 0}-\beta\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d>0}\right)}{\ell(\theta,\theta_{0})+y_{m}^{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.-\mathbb{E}\left(\frac{\xi_{i}\left(\alpha_{m}\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d_{m}\leq 0}+\beta_{m}\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d_{m}>0}-\alpha\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d\leq 0}-\beta\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d>0}\right)}{\ell(\theta,\theta_{0})+y_{m}^{2}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\left[\frac{\xi_{i}\left(\alpha_{m}\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d_{m}\leq 0}+\beta_{m}\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d_{m}>0}-\alpha\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d\leq 0}-\beta\mathds{1}_{X_{i}^{\top}d>0}\right)}{\ell(\theta,\theta_{0})+y_{m}^{2}}\right]\hskip 14.45377pt[\text{As }\mathbb{E}(\xi)=0]

That
X_{i,\theta}
is symmetric follows from the symmetry of
\xi
. We define
M
as:

\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\sup_{\theta\in\Omega\times S^{p-1}_{m}}X_{i,\theta}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\xi_{i}^{2}}{n^{2}y_{m}^{4}}:=M

and the wimpy variance:

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Omega\times S^{p-1}_{m}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}(X_{i,\theta}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{n}\frac{\ell(\theta_{m},\theta_{0})}{(\ell(\theta_{m},\theta_{0})+y_{m}^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{n}\sup_{x\geq 0}\frac{x}{(x+y_{m}^{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{4ny_{m}^{2}}:=\sigma_{m}^{2}\,.

An application of Theorem 11.17 and Theorem 11.1 of
[
9
]
yields:

\displaystyle{\sf var}(\Gamma_{m,1})\leq\sigma_{m}^{2}+64\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[M_{m}]}\mathbb{E}[\Gamma_{m,1}]+18^{2}\mathbb{E}[M_{m}]\,.

(A.44)

Note that we set
V_{m}=m(\log p)^{1+\delta}
(which is slightly larger than the VC dimension) and choose
y_{m}^{2}
as:

y_{m}^{2}=2\frac{V_{m}\|\xi\|_{2,n}}{n}\log{\frac{n}{V_{m}}}=2\text{pen}(m)\,.

As per Assumption
3.13
, we confine the model selection in
1\leq m\leq(1/4)\lfloor n/(\log{p})^{2}\rfloor
. To facilitate the union, we next show that
\sum_{i=1}^{\mathcal{M}}{\sf var}(V_{m,1})\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. We bound each terms on RHS of equation (
A.44
):

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\mathcal{M}}\sigma_{m}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{4}\sum_{i=1}^{\mathcal{M}}\frac{1}{ny_{m}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{4\|\xi\|_{n,2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n/(4(\log p)^{2})}\frac{1}{V_{m}\log{\frac{n}{V_{m}}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{4\log{4}\|\xi\|_{n,2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n/4(\log p)^{2}}\frac{1}{V_{m}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{4\log{4}(\log{p})^{\delta/2}\|\xi\|_{n,2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n/(4(\log p)^{2})}\frac{1}{m(\log{m})^{1+\delta/2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sigma_{\xi}^{2}}{4\log{4}(\log{p})^{\delta/2}\|\xi\|_{n,2}}\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{m(\log{m})^{1+\delta/2}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow 0\text{ as }n\to\infty.

Now for the second summand:

\displaystyle 64\sum_{i=1}^{\mathcal{M}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[M_{m}]}\mathbb{E}[\Gamma_{m,1}]

\displaystyle\leq 16\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[M_{m}]}

\displaystyle\lesssim 8\sum_{i=1}^{\mathcal{M}}\sqrt{\frac{\mathbb{E}[\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\xi_{i}^{2}]}{n^{2}y_{m}^{4}}}

\displaystyle=8\sum_{i=1}^{\mathcal{M}}\frac{\|\xi\|_{n,2}}{ny_{m}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8}{4\log{4}(\log{p})^{\delta/2}}\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{m(\log{m})^{1+\delta/2}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow 0\text{ as }n\to\infty.

And similarly for the third summand:

\displaystyle 18^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{\mathcal{M}}\mathbb{E}[M_{m}]

\displaystyle=18^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{\mathcal{M}}\frac{\mathbb{E}[\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\xi_{i}^{2}]}{n^{2}y_{m}^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{18^{2}}{4\log{4}(\log{p})^{\delta}}\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{m^{2}(\log{m})^{2+\delta}}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow 0\text{ as }n\to\infty.

Hence taking
t=1/8
and using the fact that
\mathbb{E}[\Gamma_{m,1}]\leq 1/8
for all
m
for our choice of
\kappa
, we have:

\mathbb{P}\left(\Gamma_{\hat{m},1}>\frac{1}{4}\right)\longrightarrow 0\,.

(A.45)

Therefore, combining equation (
A.42
) and (
A.45
) we conclude:

\mathbb{P}\left(\Gamma_{\hat{m}}>\frac{1}{2}\right)\longrightarrow 0

Hence, on its complement event, we have

\displaystyle\ell(\hat{\theta}_{\hat{m}},\theta_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\left(\ell(\hat{\theta}_{\hat{m}},d_{0})+y_{\hat{m}}^{2}\right)+\text{pen}(s_{0})-\text{pen}(\hat{m})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\ell(\hat{\theta}_{\hat{m}},d_{0})+\text{pen}(s_{0})\,,

which further implies,

\ell(\hat{\theta}_{\hat{m}},\theta_{0})\leq 2\text{pen}(s_{0})\,.

This, along with equation (
A.40
) indicates:

{\sf dist}^{2}\left((\hat{\alpha}_{\text{init}},\hat{\beta}_{\text{init}},\hat{d}),(\alpha_{0},\beta_{0},d_{0})\right)=O_{p}\left(\frac{s_{0}(\log{p})^{(1+\delta)}\|\xi\|_{n,2}}{n}\left(\log{\frac{n}{s_{0}\log{p}}}\right)\right)\,.

The boosting of the rate of
\hat{\alpha},\hat{\beta}
by replacing
d_{0}
by
\hat{d}
in the model equation is exactly same as that of Theorem
3.6
and hence skipped.

A.10
Proof of Theorem
3.12

For the proof of this theorem we follow the techniques of proof of Theorem 2.18 of
[
24
]
. Recall Fano’s inequality: if
\Theta\subseteq S^{p-1}
is a finite
2{\epsilon}
packing set, i.e. for any two
d_{i},d_{j}\in\Theta
, we have
\|d_{i}-d_{j}\|\geq 2{\epsilon}
with
|\Theta|<\infty
, then based on n i.i.d. observations
z_{1},\dots,z_{n}
we have the following minimax lower bound in estimating
d_{0}
:

\inf_{\hat{d}}\sup_{\mathcal{P}_{d}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{d}-d\right\|^{2}\right]\geq{\epsilon}^{2}\left(1-\frac{\frac{n}{M^{2}}\sum_{i,j:d_{i},d_{j}\in\Theta}KL(P_{d_{i}}||P_{d_{j}})+\log{2}}{\log{(|\Theta|-1)}}\right)

Next recall Gilbert-Varshamov Lemma: if
d_{H}
is the Hamming distance, i.e.
d_{H}(x,y)=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathds{1}(x_{i}\neq y_{i})
with
d
being the ambient dimension. Then given any
v
with
1\leq v\leq p/8
, we can find
\omega_{1},\dots,\omega_{M}\in\{0,1\}^{p}
which satisfy the following:

a)

d_{H}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\geq\frac{v}{2}\ \ \forall\ i\neq j\ \ \in\{1,\dots,m\}
.

b)

\log{M}\geq\frac{v}{8}\log{\left(1+\frac{d}{2v}\right)}
.

c)

\|\omega_{j}\|_{0}=v\ \ \forall\ j\ \in\{1,\dots,M\}
.

We choose the appropriate
{\epsilon}
later. First, for a fixed
0<{\epsilon}<1
, we construct the set
\Theta
as follows: applying Gilbert-Varshamov Lemma in dimension
p-1
with sparsity
v=s-1
, we choose
\Omega=\left\{\omega_{1},\dots,\omega_{M}\right\}\in\{0,1\}^{p-1}
which satisfies the above conditions (a) - (c). Then, for each
\omega_{j}\in\Omega
set
d_{j}
as:

d_{j}=\frac{\left(1,\frac{{\epsilon}}{\sqrt{s-1}}\omega_{j}\right)}{\sqrt{1+{\epsilon}^{2}}}

It is immediate that
\|d_{j}\|_{2}=1
and
\|d_{j}\|_{0}=s
. Set
\Theta=\{d_{j}:\omega_{j}\in\Omega\}
. From condition (c) above we have:

\left|\Theta\right|:=M\geq\frac{s-1}{8}\log{\left(1+\frac{p-1}{s-1}\right)}\,.

Further note that for any
d_{i}\neq d_{j}\in\Theta
:

\|d_{i}-d_{j}\|^{2}_{2}=\frac{{\epsilon}^{2}}{(s-1)(1+{\epsilon}^{2})}\|\omega_{i}-\omega_{j}\|^{2}=\frac{{\epsilon}^{2}}{(s-1)(1+{\epsilon}^{2})}d_{H}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})=\frac{{\epsilon}^{2}}{2(1+{\epsilon}^{2})}\geq\frac{{\epsilon}^{2}}{4}\,.

which proves that
\Theta
is a
{\epsilon}/2
packing set of
S^{p-1}
. On the other hand, from condition (c) above it is immediate that, for any
\omega_{i}\neq\omega_{j}\in\Omega
, we have
d_{H}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\leq 2s
. This implies that for any
d_{i},d_{j}\in\Theta
:

\|d_{i}-d_{j}\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{{\epsilon}^{2}}{(s-1)(1+{\epsilon}^{2})}d_{H}(\omega_{i},\omega_{j})\leq 2{\epsilon}^{2}\,.

Now for each
d_{i}\in\Theta
define the distribution
P_{d_{i}}
of
(X,Y)
as:
X\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{p}),\xi\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
,
X
is independent of
\xi
and:

Y\overset{d}{=}\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d_{i}>0}+\xi\,.

Hence for any
d_{i}\neq d_{j}\in\Theta
, the Kullback-Liebler divergence between
P_{d_{i}}
and
P_{d_{j}}
is:

\displaystyle KL(P_{d_{i}}||P_{d_{j}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{X}\left[\left(\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d_{i}>0}-\mathds{1}_{X^{\top}d_{j}>0}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\text{sign}(X^{\top}d_{i})\neq\text{sign}(X^{\top}d_{j})\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\|d_{i}-d_{j}\|_{2}\leq{\epsilon}\ \sqrt{2C^{2}}\,.

for some universal constant
C
. Hence applying Fano’s inequality we obtain:

\displaystyle\inf_{\hat{d}}\sup_{\mathcal{P}_{d}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\hat{d}-d\right\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{{\epsilon}^{2}}{16}\left(1-\frac{\frac{n}{M^{2}}\sum_{i,j:d_{i},d_{j}\in\Theta}KL(P_{d_{i}}||P_{d_{j}})+\log{2}}{\log{(|\Theta|-1)}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{{\epsilon}^{2}}{16}\left(1-\frac{n{\epsilon}\ \sqrt{2C^{2}}+\log{2}}{\log{\left(\frac{s-1}{8}\log{\left(1+\frac{p-1}{s-1}\right)}-1\right)}}\right)\,.

Taking
{\epsilon}=(s\log{(1+p/s)})/n
we conclude the proof.

Appendix B
Proof of supplementary lemmas

B.1
Proof of Lemma
A.8

As
\xi
has symmetric distribution around origin, without loss of generality we can assume
\mu>0
. Hence we have to establish the result for
0<\mu k
. Note that difference
H_{k}(\xi+\mu)-H_{k}(\xi)
can be decomposed into five terms, depending where
\xi
lies:

H_{k}(\xi+\mu)-H_{k}(\xi)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{2}\left[(\xi+\mu)^{2}-\xi^{2}\right]\,,&\text{ if }-k\leq\xi\leq k-\mu\\
\frac{1}{2}(\xi+\mu)^{2}-k\left(|\xi|-\frac{k}{2}\right)\,,&\text{ if }-k-\mu\leq\xi\leq-k\\
k\left(|\xi+\mu|-\frac{k}{2}\right)-\frac{\xi^{2}}{2}\,,&\text{ if }k-\mu\leq\xi\leq k\\
K\left(|\xi+\mu|-|\xi|\right)\,,&\text{ if }\xi>k\text{ or }\xi<-k-\mu\end{cases}

(B.1)

Now we inspect the regions individually. Note that when
-k-\mu\leq\xi\leq-k
, we have:

\displaystyle H_{k}(\xi+\mu)-H_{k}(\xi)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(\xi+\mu)^{2}-k\left(|\xi|-\frac{k}{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(\xi+\mu)^{2}+k\left(\xi+\frac{k}{2}\right)\hskip 14.45377pt[\because|\xi|=-\xi]

\displaystyle=\frac{\xi^{2}}{2}+(\mu+k)\xi+\frac{\mu^{2}+k^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\xi+\mu+k\right)^{2}-\mu k

When
k-\mu\leq\xi\leq k
:

\displaystyle H_{k}(\xi+\mu)-H_{k}(\xi)

\displaystyle=k\left(|\xi+\mu|-\frac{k}{2}\right)-\frac{\xi^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle=k\left((\xi+\mu)-\frac{k}{2}\right)-\frac{\xi^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\xi^{2}}{2}+k\xi-\frac{k^{2}}{2}+k\mu

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\xi-k\right)^{2}+k\mu

Also, we have:

k\left(|\xi+\mu|-|\xi|\right)=\begin{cases}k\mu\,,&\text{ if }\xi>k\\
-k\mu\,,&\text{ if }\xi<-k-\mu\,.\end{cases}

Hence we can modify equation (
B.1
) as:

H_{k}(\xi+\mu)-H_{k}(\xi)=\begin{cases}\mu\xi+\frac{\mu^{2}}{2}\,,&\text{ if }-k\leq\xi\leq k-\mu\\
\frac{1}{2}\left(\xi+\mu+k\right)^{2}-\mu k\,,&\text{ if }-k-\mu\leq\xi\leq-k\\
-\frac{1}{2}\left(\xi-k\right)^{2}+k\mu\,,&\text{ if }k-\mu\leq\xi\leq k\\
k\mu\,,&\text{ if }\xi>k\\
-k\mu\,,&\text{ if }\xi<-k-\mu\,.\end{cases}

(B.2)

Note that the term
-\mu k
is active on the region
\xi\leq-k
and
\mu k
is active on the region
\xi\geq k-\mu
. From the symmetry of the distribution of
\xi
, this effect of
-\mu k
and
\mu k
on the region
(-\infty,-k)
and
(k,\infty)
will cancel each other upon taking expectation and the effect of
\mu k
on
(k-\mu,k)
will remain. Hence we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[H_{k}(\xi+\mu)-H_{k}(\xi)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu^{2}}{2}\mathbb{P}\left(-k\leq\xi\leq k-\mu\right)+\mu\mathbb{E}\left[\xi\mathds{1}_{-k\leq\xi\leq k-\mu}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(\xi+\mu+k\right)^{2}\mathds{1}_{-k-\mu\leq\xi\leq-k}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[-\frac{1}{2}\left(\xi-k\right)^{2}\mathds{1}_{k-\mu\leq\xi\leq k}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu^{2}}{2}\mathbb{P}\left(-k\leq\xi\leq k-\mu\right)-\mu\mathbb{E}\left[\xi\mathds{1}_{k-\mu\leq\xi\leq k}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(\xi+\mu+k\right)^{2}\mathds{1}_{-k-\mu\leq\xi\leq-k}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mu k-\frac{1}{2}\left(\xi-k\right)^{2}\right)\mathds{1}_{k-\mu\leq\xi\leq k}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu^{2}}{2}\mathbb{P}\left(-k\leq\xi\leq k-\mu\right)+\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mu k-\mu\xi-\frac{1}{2}\left(\xi-k\right)^{2}\right)\mathds{1}_{k-\mu\leq\xi\leq k}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(\xi+\mu+k\right)^{2}\mathds{1}_{-k-\mu\leq\xi\leq-k}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mu^{2}}{2}\mathbb{P}\left(-k\leq\xi\leq k-\mu\right)+\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\mu k-\mu\xi-\frac{1}{2}\left(\xi-k\right)^{2}\right)\mathds{1}_{k-\mu\leq\xi\leq k}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mu^{2}}{2}\mathbb{P}\left(-k\leq\xi\leq k-\mu\right)

where the last inequality follows from the fact:

f(\xi)=\mu k-\mu\xi-\frac{1}{2}\left(\xi-k\right)^{2}\geq 0\ \ \ \forall\ \ \ \xi\in\left[k-\mu,k\right]\,.

observing the fact that:

\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{H}_{k}(\xi+\mu)-\tilde{H}_{k}(\xi)\right]=\frac{k+1}{k}\mathbb{E}\left[H_{k}(\xi+\mu)-H_{k}(\xi)\right]\geq\mathbb{E}\left[H_{k}(\xi+\mu)-H_{k}(\xi)\right]

we complete the proof for all
k>0
. Now for
k=0
for
0<\mu<\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|\xi+\mu|-|\xi|\right]

\displaystyle=-\mu\mathbb{P}(\xi\leq-\mu)+\mu\mathbb{P}(\xi>0)+\mathbb{E}\left[(2\xi+\mu)\mathds{1}_{-\mu\leq\xi\leq 0}\right]

\displaystyle=\mu\mathbb{P}(0\leq\xi\leq\mu)+\mathbb{E}\left[(-2\xi+\mu)\mathds{1}_{0\leq\xi\leq\mu}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[(-2\xi+2\mu)\mathds{1}_{0\leq\xi\leq\mu}\right]

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{\mu}(\mu-x)f_{\xi}(x)\ dx

\displaystyle\geq f_{\xi}(0)\int_{0}^{\mu}(\mu-x)\ dx

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu^{2}}{2}f_{\xi}(0)\,.

This completes the proof.

B.2
Proof of Lemma
A.1

Proof.

Although we assume continuous steps, our proof can be certainly extended to the case when
S_{n}
takes value
0
with positive probability. The proof critically uses Theorem 4 of Chapter 12 of Volume 2 of
[
14
]
. To keep the notational similarity with the book, define:

q_{n}=\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq n}S_{i}<0\right)

and the corresponding generating function
q(s)
as:

q(s)=1+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}s^{n}q_{n}\,.

Then from equation (7.22) of Theorem 4, Chapter 12, Vol. 2 of
[
14
]
we have:

\log{q(s)}=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{s^{n}}{n}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{n}<0\right):=f(s)\iff q(s)=e^{f(s)}\,.

(B.3)

Now we need a lower bound on
q_{n}
. Note that from the property of the generating function we have:

q_{n}=n!q^{(n)}(0)\,.

On the other hand from Faa di Bruno’s formula:

\displaystyle q^{(n)}(0)=\left.\frac{d^{n}}{ds^{n}}e^{f(s)}\right|_{s=0}

\displaystyle=\left.\left[e^{f(s)}\sum\frac{n!}{m_{1}!1!^{m_{2}}m_{1}!2!^{m_{2}}\cdots m_{n}!n!^{m_{n}}}\Pi_{j=1}^{n}\left(f^{(j)}(s)\right)^{m_{j}}\right]\right|_{s=0}

\displaystyle=\left.\left[e^{f(s)}\sum\frac{n!}{m_{1}!m_{2}!\cdots m_{n}!}\Pi_{j=1}^{n}\left(\frac{f^{(j)}(s)}{j!}\right)^{m_{j}}\right]\right|_{s=0}

\displaystyle=\sum\frac{n!}{m_{1}!m_{2}!\cdots m_{n}!}\Pi_{j=1}^{n}\left(\frac{f^{(j)}(0)}{j!}\right)^{m_{j}}

(B.4)

where the sum runs over all the sequences
\{m_{j}\}_{j=1}^{n}
such that:

\sum_{i=1}^{n}im_{i}=n\,.

Note that
f^{(j)}(0)
is non-negative for
j
. Hence using only one sequence with
m_{1}=\dots=m_{n-1}=0
and
m_{n}=1
, equation (
B.4
) can lower bounded as:

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d^{n}}{ds^{n}}e^{f(s)}\right|_{s=0}\geq f^{(n)}(0)\,.

On the from the expression of
f(s)
from equation (
B.3
) it is immediate that:

f^{(n)}(0)=n!\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{n}<0\right)\,.

Combining our findings we have:

\displaystyle n!q_{n}=q^{(n)}(0)

\displaystyle=\left.\frac{d^{n}}{ds^{n}}e^{f(s)}\right|_{s=0}

\displaystyle\geq f^{(n)}(0)

\displaystyle=n!\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{n}<0\right)\,.

This immediately implies
q_{n}\geq\mathbb{P}\left(S_{n}>0\right)/n
which completes our proof.
∎

B.3
Proof of Lemma
A.2

From the definition of distribution of
\xi
we have:

F_{\xi}(t)=\frac{\frac{1}{2}+t^{\gamma}}{1+t^{\gamma}}\ \ \ \ t\geq 0\,.

and

F_{\xi}(-t)=1-F_{\xi}(t)=\frac{1}{2(1+t^{\gamma})}\,.

Hence it is immediate that for any
t_{0}>0
:

\frac{1}{2(1+t_{0}^{-\gamma})}\leq\sup_{t\geq t_{0}}t^{\gamma}\bar{F}_{\xi}(t)=\sup_{t\geq t_{0}}\frac{t^{\gamma}}{2(1+t^{\gamma})}\leq\frac{1}{2}

(B.5)

For any fixed
k\geq 1
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(M\geq k\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geq k}\mathbb{P}\left(M=j\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geq k}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{i}>S_{j}\ \forall\ 0\leq i\leq j-1,S_{i}>S_{j}\ \forall\ i\geq j+1\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(S_{1}>0,S_{2}>0,\dots\right)\sum_{j\geq k}\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq j}S_{i}<0\right)

\displaystyle=p^{*}\sum_{j\geq k}\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq j}S_{j}<0\right)\hskip 14.45377pt[p^{*}=P\left(S_{1}>0,S_{2}>0,\dots\right)]

\displaystyle\geq p^{*}\sum_{j\geq k}\frac{1}{j}P\left(S_{j}\leq 0\right)

(B.6)

where the last inequality uses Lemma
A.1
. From the symmetry of the distribution of
\xi
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(S_{j}\leq 0\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{j}\xi_{j}\leq-j\mu\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{j}\xi_{j}>j\mu\right)\,.

Set
a_{j}=j^{1/\gamma}
. Define the event
A_{i}
as:

A_{i}=\left\{\xi_{i}>j\mu+(j-1)a_{j},\ \xi_{l}\in[-a_{j},j\mu)\ \forall\ 1\leq l\neq i\leq j\right\}

Clearly
\{A_{i}\}^{\prime}s
are disjoint events and

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{j}\xi_{j}>j\mu\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(\cup_{i=1}^{j}A_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{j}\mathbb{P}(A_{i})

\displaystyle=j\bar{F}\left(j\mu+(j-1)a_{j}\right)\left(F[-a_{j},j\mu)\right)^{j-1}

\displaystyle=j\bar{F}\left(j\mu+(j-1)a_{j}\right)\left(1-\bar{F}(a_{j})-\bar{F}(j\mu)\right)^{j-1}

(B.7)

Next note that,
j\mu+(j-1)a_{j}\geq\mu
for all
j\geq 1
. Therefore from equation (
B.5
) we have for all
j\geq 1
:

\bar{F}\left(j\mu+(j-1)a_{j}\right)\geq\frac{\left(j\mu+(j-1)a_{j}\right)^{-\gamma}}{2(1+\mu^{-\gamma})}\,,

which further implies:

\displaystyle j\times\bar{F}\left(j\mu+(j-1)a_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{{2(1+\mu^{-\alpha})}}\frac{j}{\left(j\mu+(j-1)a_{j}\right)^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{2(1+\mu^{-\gamma})}}\frac{j}{\left(j\mu+(j-1)j^{1/\alpha}\right)^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{2(1+\mu^{-\gamma})}}\frac{j}{j^{\gamma+1}\left(j^{-1/\gamma}\mu+\left(1-\frac{1}{j}\right)\right)^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{j^{\gamma}}\ \frac{1}{{2(1+\mu^{-\gamma})}}\ \frac{1}{\left(j^{-1/\gamma}\mu+\left(1-\frac{1}{j}\right)\right)^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{j^{\gamma}}\ \frac{1}{{2(1+\mu^{-\gamma})}}\ \frac{1}{(\mu+1)^{\gamma}}:=\frac{c_{1}}{j^{\gamma}}\,.

Next observe that
(j\mu)^{\gamma}\geq j
for all
j\geq 1
if
\mu>1
or for all
j\geq\mu^{-\gamma/(\gamma-1)}
if
\mu\leq 1
. Using this in equation (
B.7
) we have for all
j\geq 1\vee\lceil\mu^{-\gamma/(\gamma-1)}\rceil
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{j}\xi_{j}>j\mu\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{1}}{j^{\gamma}}\left[\left(1-\bar{F}(a_{j})-\bar{F}(j\mu)\right)^{j-1}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{1}}{j^{\gamma}}\times\left(1-\frac{1}{2(1+a_{j}^{\alpha})}-\frac{1}{2(1+(j\mu)^{\alpha})}\right)^{j-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{1}}{j^{\gamma}}\times\left(1-\frac{1}{2(1+j)}-\frac{1}{2(1+(j\mu)^{\alpha})}\right)^{j-1}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{1}}{j^{\gamma}}\times\left(1-\frac{1}{(1+j)}\right)^{j-1}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{1}}{j^{\gamma}}\times\inf_{x\geq 1}\left(1-\frac{1}{(1+x)}\right)^{x-1}

\displaystyle:=\frac{c_{1}c_{2}}{j^{\gamma}}

(B.8)

Using this in equation (
B.6
) we obtain:

\displaystyle P\left(M\geq k\right)

\displaystyle\geq p^{*}\sum_{j\geq k}\frac{1}{j}P\left(S_{j}\leq 0\right)

\displaystyle=p^{*}\sum_{j\geq k}\frac{1}{j}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{j}\xi_{j}>j\mu\right)

\displaystyle\geq c_{1}c_{2}\times p^{*}\times\sum_{j\geq k}j^{-(\gamma+1)}

\displaystyle\geq c_{1}c_{2}\times p^{*}\times\int_{k}^{\infty}x^{-(\gamma+1)}\ dx

\displaystyle\geq c_{1}c_{2}\times p^{*}\times\frac{1}{\gamma k^{\gamma}}\,.

This completes the proof of lower bound.

B.4
Proof of Lemma
A.3

We have, by symmetry:

\mathbb{P}\left(|M_{ts}|>k\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(M_{ts}>k\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(M_{ts}<-k\right)=2\mathbb{P}\left(M_{ts}>k\right)\,.

Hence, by virtue of Lemma
A.2
, all we need to show is:

P(M_{ts}=k)\geq p^{*}\mathbb{P}(M_{os}=k)\,.

Towards that end:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(M_{ts}=k\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(S_{K}\leq S_{i}\ \forall\ 0\leq i\leq k-1,S_{k}\leq S_{i}\ \forall\ i\geq k+1,S_{k}\leq\inf_{j\geq 1}S_{-j}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(S_{K}\leq S_{i}\ \forall\ 0\leq i\leq k-1,S_{k}\leq\inf_{j\geq 1}S_{-j}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(S_{i}\geq 0\ \forall\ i\geq 1\right)

\displaystyle=p^{*}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{K}\leq S_{i}\ \forall\ 0\leq i\leq k-1,S_{k}\leq\inf_{j\geq 1}S_{-j}\right)

\displaystyle\geq p^{*}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{K}\leq S_{i}\ \forall\ 0\leq i\leq k-1,S_{k}\leq\inf_{j\geq 1}S_{-j}\mid\inf_{j\geq 1}S_{-j}>0\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\inf_{j\geq 1}S_{-j}>0\right)

\displaystyle=p^{*}\mathbb{P}\left(\inf_{j\geq 1}S_{-j}>0\right)\mathbb{P}\left(S_{K}\leq S_{i}\ \forall\ 0\leq i\leq k-1\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\inf_{j\geq 1}S_{-j}>0\right)\mathbb{P}\left(M_{os}=k\right)

\displaystyle=p^{*}\mathbb{P}\left(M_{os}=k\right)\,.

where the last equality follows from the fact:

\mathbb{P}\left(M_{os}=k\right)=p^{*}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{K}\leq S_{i}\ \forall\ 0\leq i\leq k-1\right)

This completes the proof.

B.5
Proof of Lemma
A.4

Using same line of arguments as in Corollary 1:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(M_{ts,CPP}=x\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(M_{ts,CPP}=x\mid N_{1}(x)=k\right)\mathbb{P}(N_{1}(x)=k)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(M_{ts}=k\right)\mathbb{P}(N_{1}(x)=k)

\displaystyle\geq p^{*}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(M_{os}=k\right)\mathbb{P}(N_{1}(x)=k)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(M_{os,CPP}=x\right)

Hence to establish Corollary
A.4
, all we need show:

\mathbb{P}\left(M_{os,CPP}>x\right)\geq\frac{c_{0}}{2f_{X}^{\gamma}(d_{0})}x^{-\gamma}

for all large
x
, where
M
is the argmin of one sided compound Poisson process, namely the minimizer of the following:

X_{+}(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{N_{1}(t)}X_{i}\,,\ \ \ t\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\,.

Now we have:

\displaystyle P(M_{os,CPP}>x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left(M_{os,CPP}>x\mid N_{1}(x)=k\right)\mathbb{P}\left(N_{1}(x)=k\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left({\arg\min}_{i\geq 0}S_{i}>k\right)\mathbb{P}\left(N_{1}(x)=k\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left({\arg\min}_{i\geq 0}S_{i}>k\right)\mathbb{P}\left(N_{1}(x)=k\right)

\displaystyle\geq c_{0}\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{\infty}k^{-\gamma}\mathbb{P}\left(N_{1}(x)=k\right)

\displaystyle=c_{0}\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-\Lambda(x)}\Lambda(x)^{k}}{k!k^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\geq c_{0}\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-\Lambda(x)}\Lambda(x)^{k}}{k!(k+1)(k+2)\dots(k+\gamma)}

\displaystyle=c_{0}\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-\Lambda(x)}\Lambda(x)^{k}}{(k+\gamma)!}

\displaystyle=c_{0}\Lambda(x)^{-\gamma}\sum_{k=k_{0}+\alpha}^{\infty}\frac{e^{-\Lambda(x)}\Lambda(x)^{k}}{k!}

\displaystyle=c_{0}\Lambda(x)^{-\gamma}\mathbb{P}\left(N_{1}(x)\geq k_{0}+\alpha\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{0}}{2}\Lambda(x)^{-\gamma}=\frac{c_{0}}{2f_{X}^{\gamma}(d_{0})}x^{-\gamma}

where the last inequality is valid as long as
{\sf med}\left(N_{1}(X)\right)\geq k_{0}+\gamma
. From
[
2
]
, we know as
N_{1}(x)\sim Poisson(xf_{X}(d_{0}))
, we have
{\sf med}\left(N_{1}(x)\right)\geq xf_{X}(d_{0})-\log{2}
. Hence the inequality is valid as long as
x\geq(k_{0}+\gamma+\log{2})/f_{X}(d_{0})
. From This completes the proof.

B.6
Proof of Lemma
A.5

Proof.

As per our model description, all the parallel change point processes are i.i.d. Therefore
n(\hat{d}_{i}-d_{0,i})
has same distribution across
1\leq i\leq m
. Therefore, we henceforth define
F_{n}
to be the distribution of
n(\hat{d}-d_{0})
and drop
i
from subscript. From the definition of change point estimator, we have:

\displaystyle n(\hat{d}-d_{0})

\displaystyle=\text{mid argmin}_{t}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\xi_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\left\{\mathds{1}_{d_{0}<X_{i}\leq d_{0}+\frac{t}{n}}\right\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\text{mid argmin}_{t}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(-\xi_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\left\{\mathds{1}_{d_{0}+\frac{t}{n}<X_{i}\leq d_{0}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\text{mid argmin}_{t}\sum_{i=1}^{N_{n,+}(t)}\left(\xi_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\mathds{1}_{t\geq 0}+\sum_{i=1}^{N_{n,-}(t)}\left(-\xi_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\mathds{1}_{t<0}\,.

Here the count processes
N_{n,+}(t)
and
N_{n,-}(t)
are defined as follows: For
t\geq 0
,

N_{n,+}(t)=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\mathds{1}_{d_{0}\leq X_{i}\leq d_{0}+\frac{t}{n}}\sim\text{Bin}\left(n,F_{X}\left(d_{0}+\frac{t}{n}\right)-F_{X}(t)\right)

and for
t<0
,

N_{n,-}(t)=\sum_{i=0}^{n}\mathds{1}_{d_{0}+\frac{t}{n}\leq X_{i}\leq d_{0}}\sim\text{Bin}\left(n,F_{X}(t)-F_{X}\left(d_{0}+\frac{t}{n}\right)\right)\,.

These processes can be though as finite sample approximation of Compound Poisson Process, where we have approximated the Poisson random variables by Binomial random variables. It is immediate that:

\displaystyle N_{n,+}(t)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathscr{L}}{\implies}\text{Pois}\left(tf(d_{0})\right)\,,

\displaystyle N_{n,-}(t)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathscr{L}}{\implies}\text{Pois}\left(-tf(d_{0})\right)\,.

We name the process as
compound Binomial process
and henceforth denote by CBP:

CBP(t)=\sum_{i=1}^{N_{n,+}(t)}\left(\xi_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\mathds{1}_{t\geq 0}+\sum_{i=1}^{N_{n,-}(t)}\left(-\xi_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\right)\mathds{1}_{t<0}

(B.9)

Hence we work with the smallest argmin instead of mid-argmin just for some technical simplicity, but all of the following analysis is valid for mid-argmin also. As will be evident later, the thickness of the tail of the distribution
F_{n}
(the distribution of
n(\hat{d}-d_{0})
) is closely related to the tail of the minimizer of a random walk with finitely many steps. Therefore, we start by establishing a lower bound on the tail of a n-step random walk
\{S_{i}\}_{i=0}^{n}
with the usual convention
S_{0}=0
and step distribution
X_{i}\overset{d}{=}\xi_{i}+1/2
. Let
Z_{n}
be the minimizer of this random walk. The random variable
Z_{n}
is supported on
\{0,1,\dots,n\}
. Then for any
0\leq k\leq n-1
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(Z_{n}>k\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left(Z_{n}=j\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{i}>S_{j}\ \forall\ 0\leq i\leq j-1,S_{i}>S_{j}\ \forall\ j+1\leq i\leq n\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}P\left(S_{i}<0\ \forall\ 1\leq i\leq j\right)\mathbb{P}\left(S_{i}>0\ \forall\ 1\leq i\leq n-j\right)

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\left(S_{i}>0\ \forall\ 1\leq i<\infty\right)\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq j}S_{i}<0\right)

\displaystyle=p^{*}\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq j}S_{j}<0\right)\hskip 14.45377pt\left[p^{*}=\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{1\leq i<\infty}S_{i}>0\right)\right]

\displaystyle\geq p^{*}\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}\frac{1}{j}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{j}\leq 0\right)

(B.10)

From equation (
B.8
) in the proof of Lemma
A.2
we conclude for
j\geq 2^{\gamma/(\gamma-1)}:=k_{0}
:

\mathbb{P}\left(S_{j}\leq 0\right)\geq\frac{c_{1}c_{2}}{j^{\gamma}}\,.

Using the above bound in equation (
B.10
) we conclude:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(Z_{n}>k\right)

\displaystyle\geq p^{*}\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}\frac{1}{j}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{j}\leq 0\right)

\displaystyle\geq p^{*}\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}\frac{1}{j}\frac{c_{1}c_{2}}{j^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=c_{1}c_{2}p^{*}\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}\frac{1}{j^{\gamma+1}}

\displaystyle\geq c_{1}c_{2}p^{*}\int_{k+1}^{n+1}x^{-(\gamma+1)}\ dx\hskip 14.45377pt[\text{Riemann integral lower bound}]

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{1}c_{2}p^{*}}{\gamma}\left[\frac{1}{(k+1)^{\gamma}}-\frac{1}{(n+1)^{\gamma}}\right]

(B.11)

Now we go back to the random variable of interest
n(\hat{d}-d_{0})
. Let
X_{(i)}
denotes the
i^{th}
order statistics of
\{X_{i}\}_{i\leq n}
. If
X_{(i)}<d_{0}<X_{(i+1)}
, then from the definition of
n(\hat{d}-d_{0})
, we have a random walk with
i
steps on the negative axis and a random walk with
n-i
steps on the positive axis. Therefore the number of steps of random walk on either side of origin is equal to the number of
X_{i}^{\prime}s
on the corresponding side of
d_{0}
. Denote by
R_{n}
(and respectively
L_{n}
), the number of
X_{i}^{\prime}s
greater than
d_{0}
(respectively less than
d_{0}
). Hence
R_{n}\sim\text{Bin}(n,\bar{F}_{X}(d_{0}))
and
L_{n}\sim\text{Bin}(n,F_{X}(d_{0}))
with
R_{n}+L_{n}=n
. Then we have for any
x>0
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(n(\hat{d}-d_{0})>x\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{n}\sum_{k=0}^{r}\mathbb{P}\left(n(\hat{d}-d_{0})>x\mid R_{n}=r,N_{n,+}(x)=k\right)\mathbb{P}(N_{n,+}(x)=k\mid R_{n}=r)\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)

Given
R_{n}=r
, we have a two sided random walk, with
r
steps on the positive real line
n-r
steps on the negative real line. Therefore, the event
n(\hat{d}-d_{0})>x
given
N_{n,+}(x)=k
and
R_{n}=r
is equivalent to the event that in a two sided random walks with
r
steps on the right and
n-r
steps on the left, the argmin is on the right and it happens after
k
steps. More precisely, if we denote by
S_{0}\equiv 0,S_{1},\dots,S_{r}
to be the random walk on the right side with step distribution
(\xi+1/2)
and
S^{\prime}_{0}\equiv 0,S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{n-r}
to be random walk on the left with step distribution
(-\xi+1/2)
, then the above event corresponds that this two sided random walk is minimized at
S_{j}
for some
k+1\leq j\leq r
. Therefore we write:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(n(\hat{d}-d_{0})>x\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{n}\sum_{k=0}^{r}\mathbb{P}\left(n(\hat{d}-d_{0})>x\mid R_{n}=r,N_{n,+}(x)=k\right)\mathbb{P}(N_{n,+}(x)=k\mid R_{n}=r)\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{n}\sum_{k=0}^{r}\left[\mathbb{P}\left({\arg\min}\text{ of twosided RW }>k\right)\times\mathbb{P}(N_{n,+}(x)=k\mid R_{n}=r)\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)\right]

(B.12)

Next, we obtain a lower bound on the tail of the minimizer of the two sided random walk. Note that, we have already established a lower bound on the tail of the minimizer of a one sided random walk in equation (
B.11
), which we exploit here to get a lower bound on the tail of the minimizer of this two-sided incarnation:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\arg\min}\text{ twosided RW }>k\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=k+1}^{r}\mathbb{P}\left({\arg\min}\text{ twosided RW }=j\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=k+1}^{r}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{j}<S_{0},\dots,S_{j}<S_{j-1},S_{j}<S_{j+1},\dots,S_{j}<S_{r},S_{j}<\min_{1\leq i\leq n-r}S^{\prime}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=k+1}^{r}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{j}<S_{0},\dots,S_{j}<S_{j-1},S_{j}<\min_{1\leq i\leq n-r}S^{\prime}_{i}\right)\mathbb{P}(S_{1}>0,\dots,S_{r-j}>0)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{j=k+1}^{r}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{j}<S_{0},\dots,S_{j}<S_{j-1},S_{j}<\min_{1\leq i\leq n-r}S^{\prime}_{i}\mid\min_{1\leq i\leq n-r}S^{\prime}_{i}>0\right)\times

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt\mathbb{P}\left(\min_{1\leq i\leq n-r}S^{\prime}_{i}>0\right)\mathbb{P}(S_{1}>0,\dots,S_{r-j}>0)

\displaystyle\geq p^{*}\sum_{j=k+1}^{r}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{j}<S_{0},\dots,S_{j}<S_{j-1},S_{j}<\min_{1\leq i\leq n-r}S^{\prime}_{i}\mid\min_{1\leq i\leq n-r}S^{\prime}_{i}>0\right)\times

\displaystyle\hskip 250.00038pt\mathbb{P}(S_{1}>0,\dots,S_{r-j}>0)

\displaystyle=p^{*}\sum_{j=k+1}^{r}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{j}<S_{0},\dots,S_{j}<S_{j-1}\right)\mathbb{P}(S_{1}>0,\dots,S_{r-j}>0)

\displaystyle=p^{*}\mathbb{P}\left({\arg\min}\text{ one-sided RW with length }r>k\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{1}c_{2}(p^{*})^{2}}{\gamma}\left[\frac{1}{(k+1)^{\gamma}}-\frac{1}{(r+1)^{\gamma}}\right]\hskip 14.45377pt[\text{From equation }\eqref{eq:finite_sample_rw_lower_bound}]\,.

For the rest of the calculation we assume
\gamma
(the number of finite moments of the error distribution
\xi
) is an integer, as all our calculation is valid by replacing
\gamma
by
\lfloor\gamma\rfloor
. Define a success probability
p_{x,n}
as:

p_{x,n}=\mathbb{P}\left(X\in\left(d_{0}+\frac{x}{n},d_{0}\right)\mid X>d_{0}\right)=\frac{F_{X}\left(d_{0}+\frac{x}{n},d_{0}\right)-F_{X}(d_{0})}{1-F_{X}(d_{0})}\,.

Therefore it is immediate that:

N_{n,+}(x)\mid R_{n}=r\sim\text{Bin}\left(r,p_{x,n}\right)\,.

As per our assumption
F_{X}
has continuous density
f_{X}
with
f_{X}(d_{0})>0
. Therefore, there exists
\delta_{1}>0
such that
f_{X}(t)>f_{X}(d_{0})/2
for
|t-d_{0}|\leq\delta_{1}
. Hence, for any
0\leq x\leq n\delta_{1}
, we have:

p_{x,n}\geq\frac{x}{n}\times\frac{f_{X}(d_{0})}{2(1-F_{X}(d_{0}))}\,.

(B.13)

On the other, let
f_{\max}
be the upper bound on
f_{X}
on the entire
\mathbb{R}
. Then, again from the mean value theorem, we have:

p_{x,n}\leq\frac{x}{n}\times\frac{f_{\max}}{2(1-F_{X}(d_{0}))}\,.

(B.14)

Therefore combining equations (
B.13
) and (
B.14
), we have:

\frac{x}{n}\times\frac{f_{X}(d_{0})}{2(1-F_{X}(d_{0}))}\leq p_{x,n}\leq\frac{x}{n}\times\frac{f_{\max}}{2(1-F_{X}(d_{0}))}\,.

(B.15)

We will use the above relations in our rest of the calculation. Going back to equation (
B.12
) we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(n(\hat{d}-d_{0})>x\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=1}^{n}\sum_{k=0}^{r}\left[\mathbb{P}\left({\arg\min}\text{ twosided RW }>k\right)\times\mathbb{P}(N_{n,+}(x)=k\mid R_{n}=r)\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=k_{0}}^{n}\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{r}\left[\mathbb{P}\left({\arg\min}\text{ twosided RW }>k\right)\times\mathbb{P}(N_{n,+}(x)=k\mid R_{n}=r)\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{1}c_{2}(p^{*})^{2}}{\gamma}\sum_{r=1}^{n}\sum_{k=0}^{r}\left[\frac{1}{(k+1)^{\gamma}}-\frac{1}{(r+1)^{\gamma}}\right]\mathbb{P}(N_{n,+}(x)=k\mid R_{n}=r)\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{1}c_{2}(p^{*})^{2}}{\gamma}\left[\sum_{r=k_{0}}^{n}\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{r}\frac{1}{(k+1)^{\gamma}}\mathbb{P}(N_{n,+}(x)=k\mid R_{n}=r)\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt\left.-\sum_{r=k_{0}}^{n}\frac{1}{(r+1)^{\gamma}}\mathbb{P}(k_{0}\leq N_{n,+}(x)<r\mid R_{n}=r)\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{1}c_{2}(p^{*})^{2}}{\gamma}\left[\sum_{r=k_{0}}^{n}\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{r}\frac{1}{(k+1)^{\gamma}}\mathbb{P}(N_{n,+}(x)=k\mid R_{n}=r)\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt\left.-\sum_{r=1}^{n}\frac{1}{(r+1)^{\gamma}}\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{1}c_{2}(p^{*})^{2}}{\gamma}\left[\sum_{r=k_{0}}^{n}\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{r}\frac{1}{(k+1)^{\gamma}}\dbinom{r}{k}p_{x,n}^{k}(1-p_{x,n})^{r-k}\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt\left.-\sum_{r=1}^{n}\frac{1}{(r+1)^{\gamma}}\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{1}c_{2}(p^{*})^{2}}{\gamma}\left[\sum_{r=k_{0}}^{n}\left\{\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{r}\frac{1}{(k+1)^{\gamma}}\dbinom{r}{k}p_{x,n}^{k}(1-p_{x,n})^{r-k}\right\}\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)-\sum_{r=1}^{n}\frac{1}{(r+1)^{\gamma}}\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)\right]

(B.16)

where the last inequality is obtained by replacing
1-p_{x,n}
by its upper bound 1. The inner sum of the above equation can be analyzed as follows:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{r}\frac{1}{(k+1)^{\gamma}}\dbinom{r}{k}p_{x,n}^{k}(1-p_{x,n})^{r-k}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{r}\frac{1}{(k+1)^{\gamma}}\frac{r!}{k!(r-k)!}p_{x,n}^{k}(1-p_{x,n})^{r-k}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{r}\frac{1}{(k+1)(k+2)\dots(k+\gamma)}\frac{r!}{k!(r-k)!}p_{x,n}^{k}(1-p_{x,n})^{r-k}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{p_{x,n}^{-\gamma}}{(r+1)(r+2)\dots(r+\gamma)}\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{r}\frac{(r+\gamma)!}{(k+\gamma)!(r-k)!}p_{x,n}^{k+\gamma}(1-p_{x,n})^{r-k}

\displaystyle=\frac{p_{x,n}^{-\gamma}}{(r+1)(r+2)\dots(r+\gamma)}\mathbb{P}\left(\text{Bin}(r+\gamma,p_{x,n})\geq k_{0}+\gamma\right)\,.

Putting this back into equation (
B.16
) we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(n(\hat{d}-d_{0})>x\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{1}c_{2}(p^{*})^{2}}{\gamma}p_{x,n}^{-\gamma}\left[\sum_{r=k_{0}}^{n}\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(\text{Bin}(r+\gamma,p_{x,n})\geq k_{0}+\gamma\right)}{(r+1)(r+2)\dots(r+\gamma)}\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)-p_{x,n}^{\gamma}\sum_{r=1}^{n}\frac{1}{(r+1)^{\gamma}}\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)\right]

(B.17)

Now from the properties of the inverse moments of the binomial distribution (see e.g.
[
11
]
) we have:

\sum_{r=1}^{n}\frac{1}{(r+1)^{\gamma}}\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)\leq C\left(n\bar{F}_{X}(d_{0})\right)^{-\gamma}\hskip 14.45377pt[\text{Recall }R_{n}\sim\text{Bin}(n,\bar{F}_{X}(d_{0}))]\,.

On the other hand we have for all
r\geq n\bar{F}_{X}(d_{0})
:

\displaystyle(r+\gamma)p_{x,n}

\displaystyle\geq(n\bar{F}_{X}(d_{0})+\gamma)\times\frac{x}{n}\times\frac{f_{X}(d_{0})}{2(1-F_{X}(d_{0}))}\hskip 14.45377pt[\text{Equation }\eqref{eq:pxn_lb}]

\displaystyle\geq\left(\bar{F}_{X}(d_{0})+\frac{\gamma}{n}\right)\times x\times\frac{f_{X}(d_{0})}{2(1-F_{X}(d_{0}))}

\displaystyle\geq\bar{F}_{X}(d_{0})\times x\times\frac{f_{X}(d_{0})}{2(1-F_{X}(d_{0}))}>\gamma+k_{0}

for all
x>2(\gamma+k_{0})/f_{X}(d_{0})
. Now we know that the median of
Bin(n,p)
is
\lfloor np\rfloor
or
\lceil np\rceil
. For simplicity, we will use the bound here
\mathbb{P}(Bin(n,p)\geq np)\geq 1/2
as it will be valid simply replacing
np
by
\lfloor np\rfloor
and this will not alter any of our subsequent analysis. Therefore we have:

\displaystyle\sum_{r=k_{0}}^{n}\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(\text{Bin}(r+\gamma,p_{x,n})\geq\gamma+k_{0}\right)}{(r+1)(r+2)\dots(r+\gamma)}\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{r=n\bar{F}(d_{0})}^{n}\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(\text{Bin}(r+\gamma,p_{x,n})\geq\gamma+k_{0}\right)}{(r+1)(r+2)\dots(r+\gamma)}\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{r=n\bar{F}(d_{0})}^{n}\frac{1}{2(r+1)(r+2)\dots(r+\gamma)}\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2(n+1)(n+2)\dots(n+\gamma)}\sum_{r=n\bar{F}(d_{0})}^{n}\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4(n+1)(n+2)\dots(n+\gamma)}\,.

Going back to equation (
B.17
) we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(n(\hat{d}-d_{0})>x\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{1}c_{2}(p^{*})^{2}}{\gamma}p_{x,n}^{-\gamma}\left[\sum_{r=k_{0}}^{n}\frac{\mathbb{P}\left(\text{Bin}(r+\gamma,p_{x,n})\geq\gamma\right)}{(r+1)(r+2)\dots(r+\gamma)}\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)-p_{x,n}^{\gamma}\sum_{r=1}^{n}\frac{1}{(r+1)^{\gamma}}\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{1}c_{2}(p^{*})^{2}}{\gamma}p_{x,n}^{-\gamma}\left[\frac{1}{4(n+1)(n+2)\dots(n+\gamma)}-p_{x,n}^{\gamma}\frac{C(\bar{F}(d_{0}))^{-\gamma}}{n^{\gamma}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{1}c_{2}(p^{*})^{2}}{\gamma}x^{\gamma}\times\left(\frac{f_{X,\max}}{1-F_{X}(d_{0})}\right)^{-\gamma}\left[\frac{n^{\gamma}}{4(n+1)(n+2)\dots(n+\gamma)}-(np_{x,n})^{\gamma}\frac{C(\bar{F}(d_{0}))^{-\gamma}}{n^{\gamma}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{1}c_{2}(p^{*})^{2}}{\gamma}x^{\gamma}\times\left(\frac{f_{X,\max}}{1-F_{X}(d_{0})}\right)^{-\gamma}\left[\frac{1}{4(2^{\gamma})}-(np_{x,n})^{\gamma}\frac{C(\bar{F}(d_{0}))^{-\gamma}}{n^{\gamma}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{1}c_{2}(p^{*})^{2}}{\gamma}x^{\gamma}\times\left(\frac{f_{X,\max}}{1-F_{X}(d_{0})}\right)^{-\gamma}\left[\frac{1}{4(2^{\gamma})}-\left(x\times\frac{f_{X,\max}}{1-F_{X}(d_{0})}\right)^{\gamma}\frac{C(\bar{F}(d_{0}))^{-\gamma}}{n^{\gamma}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{1}c_{2}(p^{*})^{2}}{\gamma}x^{\gamma}\times\left(\frac{f_{X,\max}}{1-F_{X}(d_{0})}\right)^{-\gamma}\left[\frac{1}{4(2^{\gamma})}-\left(\frac{\delta_{2}f_{X,\max}}{C(1-F_{X}(d_{0}))^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}\right]\hskip 14.45377pt[\forall\ x\leq\delta_{2}n]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{c_{1}c_{2}(p^{*})^{2}}{\gamma}\times\left(\frac{f_{X,\max}}{1-F_{X}(d_{0})}\right)^{-\gamma}\times\frac{1}{2^{\gamma+3}}\times x^{-\gamma}

\displaystyle=\frac{c_{1}c_{2}(p^{*})^{2}}{\gamma 2^{\gamma+3}}\times\left(\frac{f_{X,\max}}{1-F_{X}(d_{0})}\right)^{-\gamma}\times x^{-\gamma}\,.

where the last inequality is valid for small enough
\delta_{2}
, i.e. we choose
\delta_{2}
which satisfies:

\frac{1}{2^{\gamma+2}}-\left(\frac{\delta_{2}f_{X,\max}}{C(1-F_{X}(d_{0}))^{2}}\right)^{\gamma}\geq\frac{1}{2^{\gamma+3}}\,.

Therefore we have established that for any
2\gamma/f_{X}(d_{0})\leq x\leq(\delta_{1}\wedge\delta_{2})n
:

\mathbb{P}\left(n(\hat{d}-d_{0})>x\right)\leq\frac{c_{1}c_{2}(p^{*})^{2}}{\gamma 2^{\gamma+3}}\times\left(\frac{f_{X,\max}}{1-F_{X}(d_{0})}\right)^{-\gamma}\times x^{-\gamma}\,.

(B.18)

The calculation for the negative
x
is similar. As introduced before,
L_{n}
denotes the number of
X_{i}^{\prime}s
on the left of
d_{0}
and
L_{n}\sim\text{Bin}(n,F(d_{0}))
. Given
L_{n}=l
, define
S_{0}\equiv 0,S^{\prime}_{1},\dots,S^{\prime}_{l}
to be random walk on the left of origin and
S_{0}=0,S_{1},S_{2},\dots,S_{n-l}
on the right of origin. Given
L_{n}=l,N_{n,-}(x)=k^{\prime}
, the event
n(\hat{d}-d_{0})<-x
is equivalent to the event that in a two sided random walk with
l
steps on the left and
n-l
steps on the right, the minima occurs on the left and it occurs at one of the steps among
\left\{S^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}+1},S^{\prime}_{k^{\prime}+2},\dots,S^{\prime}_{l}\right\}
. Using the similar logic as above we obtain for any
2\gamma/f_{X}(d_{0})\leq x\leq(\delta_{1}\wedge\delta_{2})n
:

\mathbb{P}\left(n(\hat{d}-d_{0})<-x\right)\geq\frac{c_{1}c_{2}(p^{*})^{2}}{\gamma 2^{\gamma+3}}\times\left(\frac{f_{X,\max}}{1-F_{X}(d_{0})}\right)^{-\gamma}\times x^{-\gamma}\,.

(B.19)

Finally, from equation (
B.18
) and (
B.19
) we conclude for any
2\gamma/f_{X}(d_{0})\leq x\leq(\delta_{1}\wedge\delta_{2})n
:

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|n(\hat{d}-d_{0})\right|>x\right)\geq\frac{c_{1}c_{2}(p^{*})^{2}}{\gamma 2^{\gamma+2}}\times\left(\frac{f_{X,\max}}{1-F_{X}(d_{0})}\right)^{-\gamma}\times x^{-\gamma}\,.

This completes the proof.
∎

B.7
Proof of Lemma
A.6

We use the same notations as used in the proof of Lemma
A.5
. As
\xi_{i}
’s are bounded by
b
with mean
\mu>0
, by applying Hoeffding’s inequality, we have for any
j\in\mathbb{N}
:

\mathbb{P}\left(\bar{\xi}_{j}<-\mu\right)\leq e^{-\frac{j\mu^{2}}{4b^{2}}}:=e^{-cj}\,,

with
c=\mu^{2}/4b^{2}
. As in Lemma
A.5
, we start with establishing an upper bound on the tail on the minimizer of random walk. Let
\{S_{j}\}_{j=0,\dots,n}
denotes a
n
-step random walk and let
Z_{n}
denotes its minimizer supported on
\{0,1,\dots,n\}
. We then have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(Z_{n}>k\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left(Z_{n}=j\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{j}<0,\dots,S_{j}<S_{j-1},S_{j}<S_{j+1},\dots,S_{j}<S_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{j}<0\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left(\bar{\xi}_{j}<-\mu\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=k+1}^{n}e^{-cj}=\frac{e^{-c(k+1)}}{1-e^{-c}}\,.

Going back to distribution of
n(\hat{d}-d_{0})
, as in the proof of Lemma
A.5
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(n(\hat{d}-d_{0})>x\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{n}\sum_{k=0}^{r}\mathbb{P}\left(n(\hat{d}-d_{0})>x\mid R_{n}=r,N_{n,+}(x)=k\right)\mathbb{P}(N_{n,+}(x)=k\mid R_{n}=r)\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{n}\sum_{k=0}^{r}\left[\mathbb{P}\left({\arg\min}\text{ twosided RW }>k\mid R_{n}=r,N_{n,+}(x)=k\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\times\mathbb{P}(N_{n,+}(x)=k\mid R_{n}=r)\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{n}\sum_{k=0}^{r}\left[\mathbb{P}\left({\arg\min}\text{ twosided RW }>k\mid R_{n}=r\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\times\mathbb{P}(N_{n,+}(x)=k\mid R_{n}=r)\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)\right]

(B.20)

Upper bounding the argmin of two-sided random walk is relatively easier:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left({\arg\min}\text{ twosided RW }>k\mid R_{n}=r\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=k+1}^{r}\mathbb{P}\left({\arg\min}\text{ twosided RW }=j\mid R_{n}=r\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=k+1}^{r}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{j}<\min_{0\leq i\leq j-1}S_{i},S_{j}<\min_{j+1\leq i\leq r}S_{i},S_{j}<\min_{1\leq i\leq n-r}S^{\prime}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=k+1}^{r}\mathbb{P}\left(S_{j}<0\right)\leq\frac{e^{-c(k+1)}}{1-e^{-c}}\,.

Using this bound in equation (
B.20
) we obtain for any
0\leq x\leq n\delta_{1}
(where
\delta_{1}
is same as defined in the proof of Lemma
A.5
, i.e. we choose
\delta_{1}>0
such that
f_{X}(t)\geq f_{X}(d_{0})/2
for all
|t-d_{0}|\leq\delta_{1}
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(n(\hat{d}-d_{0})>x\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{n}\sum_{k=0}^{r}\left[\mathbb{P}\left({\arg\min}\text{ twosided RW }>k\mid R_{n}=r\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.\times\mathbb{P}(N_{n,+}(x)=k\mid R_{n}=r)\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{r=0}^{n}\sum_{k=0}^{r}\frac{e^{-c(k+1)}}{1-e^{-c}}\mathbb{P}(N_{n,+}(x)=k\mid R_{n}=r)\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e^{-c}}{1-e^{-c}}\sum_{r=0}^{n}\sum_{k=0}^{r}e^{-ck}\ \mathbb{P}(N_{n,+}(x)=k\mid R_{n}=r)\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-c}}{1-e^{-c}}\sum_{r=0}^{n}\left(1-p_{n,x}+p_{n,x}e^{-c}\right)^{r}\mathbb{P}(R_{n}=r)

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-c}}{1-e^{-c}}\left(1-\bar{F}(d_{0})+\bar{F}(d_{0})\left(1-p_{n,x}+p_{n,x}e^{-c}\right)\right)^{n}

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{-c}}{1-e^{-c}}\left(1-\bar{F}(d_{0})p_{n,x}(1-e^{-c})\right)^{n}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e^{-c}}{1-e^{-c}}\left(1-(1-e^{-c})\frac{xf_{X}(d_{0})}{2n}\right)^{n}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{e^{-c}}{1-e^{-c}}e^{-x\frac{f_{X}(d_{0})}{2}(1-e^{-c})}\,.

The calculation for
\mathbb{P}(n(\hat{d}-d_{0})<-x)
for
x>0
is similar and hence skipped for brevity. Therefore we obtain for
0\leq|x|\leq n\delta_{1}
:

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|n(\hat{d}-d_{0})\right|>x\right)\leq\frac{2e^{-c}}{1-e^{-c}}e^{-x\frac{f_{X}(d_{0})}{2}(1-e^{-c})}\,.

This completes the proof.

B.8
Proof of Proposition
A.7

From proposition 5 of
[
15
]
we have:

\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi f(X_{i})\right\|_{\mathcal{F}}\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\left|\eta_{(k)}\right|-\left|\eta_{(k+1)}\right|\right)\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}{\epsilon}_{i}f(X_{i})\right\|_{\mathcal{F}}\right]

where
\left|\eta_{(1)}\right|\geq\left|\eta_{(2)}\right|\geq\dots\left|\eta_{(n)}\right|\geq\left|\eta_{(n+1)}\right|=0
are the decreasing order statistics of
\left\{\left|\xi_{i}-\xi_{i}^{\prime}\right|\right\}_{i=1}^{n}
where
\left\{\xi_{i}^{\prime}\right\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
are i.i.d copy of
\left\{\xi_{i}\right\}_{1\leq i\leq n}
. Hence we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi f(X_{i})\right\|_{\mathcal{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\left|\eta_{(k)}\right|-\left|\eta_{(k+1)}\right|\right)\mathbb{E}\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{k}{\epsilon}_{i}f(X_{i})\right\|_{\mathcal{F}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{n}\left(\left|\eta_{(k)}\right|-\left|\eta_{(k+1)}\right|\right)\left(\varphi_{n}(k)+b_{n}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\int_{\left|\eta_{(k+1)}\right|}^{\left|\eta_{(k)}\right|}\varphi_{n}(k)\ dt\right]+b_{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\eta_{(1)}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{\infty}\varphi_{n}\left(\left|\left\{i:\left|\eta_{i}\right|\geq t\right\}\right|\right)\ dt\right]+b_{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\left|\xi_{i}-\xi_{i}^{\prime}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\varphi_{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left(|\xi_{i}-\xi_{i}^{\prime}|>t\right)\right)+2b_{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\left|\xi_{i}\right|\right]\hskip 14.45377pt[\text{By Jensen's inequality}]

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}\varphi_{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\mathbb{P}\left(|\xi_{i}|>t/2\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(|\xi^{\prime}_{i}|>t/2\right)\right)\right)+2b_{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\left|\xi_{i}\right|\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\varphi_{n}\left(2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left(|\xi_{i}|>t/2\right)\right)+2b_{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\left|\xi_{i}\right|\right]

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{\infty}\varphi_{n}\left(2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left(|\xi_{i}|>t\right)\right)+2b_{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\left|\xi_{i}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq 4\int_{0}^{\infty}\varphi_{n}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{P}\left(|\xi_{i}|>t\right)\right)+2b_{n}\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{1\leq i\leq n}\left|\xi_{i}\right|\right]

where the last inequality follows from the fact that
\varphi_{n}(0)=0
and
\varphi_{n}
concave which leads to
\varphi_{n}(2x)\leq 2\varphi_{n}(x)
.
